# Quant experiments

Every experiment in the quants subproject, planned or done, so that nothing is run twice. Check this page before
starting anything; add a row before an experiment starts and fill it in when it ends. Results are summarized here and
written up in [README.md](README.md) or [research.md](research.md); raw logs are kept outside the repository.

## Plan

**Quality bar (decided 2026-10-23):** a Flint file counts only if it is within noise of Unsloth's UD-Q4_K_XL against
BF16 (KLD 0.120, 87.7 % same top token, perplexity +3.9 %; in practice KLD ≲ 0.125 and same top token ≳ 87.5 %).
People use Unsloth's quants, and nobody trades ~9 points of token agreement for speed, so files at the makers' Q4_1
level (78–80 %: makers-fast, S1, S2, the Q4_0 variants) are no longer release candidates or targets. Speed claims compare
against UD-Q4_K_XL on the same build and cards. Engine and kernel work still counts wherever it is measured, because it
speeds up every file.

**Now (2026-11-09):** v3.0 is released (2026-11-06). Flash-Next stays the lead model. The order of the next work is
section 8 of [the second review](../docs/research/notes/flash_next_review2_2026-11.md), which checks and revises
[the first](../docs/research/notes/flash_next_review_2026-11.md). The headline becomes minutes per solved real case
(Q1163). On one rig: the step's map (the per-part profile is done, Q1191: a four-token pass is 85.36 ms of GPU time in a 91.20 ms pass; the served step is done too, Q961: 131.57 ms at shallow depth, 115.76 ms of a 133.18 ms mean step GPU time, the server's 7.84 ms before each verify pass the one host part above 5 ms), probabilistic verification with a
kept-draws arm (Q731, Q1160), the prompt lane and one prompt's timeline (Q1155, Q1156). On the other: the sparse
attention (Q230; timed on the deep lane's first rig pair, Q1143: a step at 49,152 tokens takes 180.77 ms with it against 301.90 without, a gain from 16,384 tokens on in tok/s and from 8,192 in the step's time), the deep lane with the draft arm (Q1143, Q1153; done: in generation the draft is not a loss at any depth up to 49,152 tokens, and it costs 28.4 to 41.3 % of prompt speed from 8,192 on), a 131,072-token line (Q1154; done: it fits and holds to 120,000 tokens with the draft, and generates 15.24 tok/s with it and 10.96 without at 120,000, one load each), several slots
(Q1164). On the quant machine: the bracket Swift Q2_0 against Swift IQ3_S (Q1165; the sister repo's E1 and E13), the
draft gates (Q1109, Q806), KLD by depth (Q1162). The loop census is done (Q1138, Q1158: no reply ends in a repeated
token, and the runaways are long reasoning, not loops), and so is the reasoning census (Q1152: 79.42 % of Flash-Next's
generated tokens are reasoning, with 2.74 reflection words per 1,000, below the ~5 line; the longest 10 % of replies
hold 63.20 % of the tokens). The draft's acceptance by depth is counted (Q1172: no fall up to 49,152 tokens; on
whole turns next, Q1176). Probabilistic verification's tokens a step are counted (Q1173: 3.283 and 3.304 against the
preset's 3.043 and 3.072, +7.74 %; kept draws change nothing on this line), and its rig run is done (Q731: generation
21.31 and 21.22 tok/s against the preset's 20.60 and 20.70, +2.97 %, a gain; tokens a step +4.04 % on the rig, half
of the count off it, Q1217; the step not measurably dearer, the extra time in its grammar rejections, Q1187). On the agent check off the rig its 12 tasks all pass and take no more turns (Q1183: 64 and 60
turns against the preset's 92 and 67; 35,406 and 36,683 generated tokens against 34,945 and 21,936, inside the
preset's own spread), and on fixed prompts its replies are not longer beyond the loads' spread (Q1184: 95,670 and 72,564 tokens for 60 replies against the preset's 66,802 and 91,163; by the source the draw after a rejection is the residual). Swift-1.5 has no MTP head of its own (Q1168: the layer in its checkpoint is the base model's, and
as a draft it gives 3.086 and 3.073 tokens a step against the draft in use's 3.043, 3.072 and 3.090), so the preset
keeps the Gyro draft. Code starts with the read of the multi-GPU fork (Q1157) and
`-ladder6` (Q1150). Nothing runs until the owner starts each row.

**Before that (2026-11-02):** Qwen3.8-Flash-Next leads, Swift-1.5 GSQ-RCO Q2_0 first, with IQ3_XXS as the quality reference: where the
n-gram table lives (Q214, Q435), the fit probe (Q213), then the baseline and agent check (Q215). Strata's techniques are the
port list (Q436–Q471, [strata.md](../docs/research/notes/strata.md)), with Q215's profile setting their order. Nothing is
running: the rig is off and the quant machine is being rebuilt.

**Until then (2026-10-27, 20:40):** v2.0 is released; v3 puts Ornith-1.5-122B first (Q158, Q164, Q166, Q167, Q170, Q171)
with Flint's MTP head alongside (Q169, Q168). The rig is off for the night; from 2026-10-28 08:00 it runs Ornith's
attention share and the round-2 IQ kernels on UD-Q2_K_XL (Q166), the cheaper MTP guess and the answer store as paired
replays, then the overnight MTP heads (Q169). The 7-GPU quant machine runs overnight: Flint's MTP heads on two RTX 3090s
until ~08:00 (Q169), the Ornith quality runs on the V100s (Q170, Q171, Q164's files c and b), Flint near-Q8_0 on the
other three cards (Q168), and Q164's files b and d on the CPU. Keep this line current: say what is running, where, and
since when. 2026-10-28: Ornith-1.5-35B's files from its BF16 are measured (Q159: P-Q5 and PK-Q4-i lead); its agent
check is planned (Q311).

### Priorities (v2)

The list is ordered by expected value for the v2 release: Flint faster than Unsloth's UD-Q4_K_XL at its quality,
measured on this rig against stock llama.cpp and against UD-Q4_K_XL on the same build.

| # | Goal | Experiments | State | Next step | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Release file:** the best quality at equal speed against UD-Q4_K_XL with the same head treatment (screened head on both). On our engine the lead is K2sr (K2 + screened Q8_0 head + F16 router, 2.053 GB per token; Q126); for stock llama.cpp K2h-r16 (2.091 GB, GPTQ head). UD-Q4_K_XL with the screened head on our engine is the candidate to beat | Q41, Q126, Q127, Q125, Q106, Q112, Q113, Q129, Q42 | KLD at 512 tokens G3h 0.106 @ 2.21 GB, K2h 0.109 @ 2.14, K1h 0.112 @ 2.10 (UD 0.120); at 8k on agent text all 1.03–1.04× UD (BF16-confirmed); E2 miss_param behind UD (exploratory); reasoning lengths level; with screened heads on both, est. no per-token edge for K2h | Q41's confirmatory miss_param test (until 12:00); Q107's agent-text scores; Q112's GPTQ groups; rig: Q125 (same head treatment) and the candidates' speed; the decision run (799 tasks × K ≥ 2, E3 at 64k) needs owner time | rig; quant machine |
| 2 | **The pipelined executor:** keep the idle cards busy, with guessed tokens, race samples or other conversations | Q87, Q104, Q93, then Q20 as a one-token drafter | simulated 1.34× untrained, 2.3× at 70 % acceptance; executor being proven correct | rig timing of the executor; if it holds, train the drafter | code; rig; quant machine |
| 3 | **Agent traffic:** tool requests turn off backend sampling and, by its rule, the screened head, so on agent work v2 decodes only ~1.10× faster per token; keep tool requests on the GPU path, and measure real agent work end to end | Q55, Q66, Q67, Q131, Q132, Q120, Q121, Q119, Q65, Q68, Q118 ✓, Q122 (parked) | every pi/OpenCode request carries tools, so sampling runs on the host with the 600 KB logits copy (est. 3.2–3.5 ms per token) and the screen is off; Q118: no server gap for plain chat; agent-check suite designed (Q131) | Q55 on the rig today (1 h); Q67 in the fork (GPU sampling with a host grammar check, est. +9–11 % on tool requests, plus the screen kept); Q131: build the suite, calibrate on the quant machine, then the rig pilot | rig; code |
| 4 | **Answer time: shorter reasoning** (two thirds of Flint's output is reasoning) | Q93, Q94, Q85 | the lossy-weight room under the bar is ~1 ms per token; shorter reasoning is the largest wall-clock lever left | Q93 from Q41's logs (no GPU), Q94's reflection count; then task checks with Q41's harness | quant machine (tasks); rig later |
| 5 | **Prompt processing** | Q108, Q109, Q110, Q116, Q111, Q89, Q80 | Q108 (PR #31047), Q109 (pipeline fill) and Q110 (on-card mask) built, tests pass, logits identical; rig tarballs ready; est. combined 130–190 instead of 56–72 generated tok/s for tool-heavy agents | rig: copies × `-ub` grid, then the prefill and gpumask builds against v2 (runbook step 5); Q116 (decode mask) after that | code; rig |
| 6 | **Engine, fixed cost and multi-token steps** | Q88, Q99, Q100, Q78, Q76 ✓, Q50 ✓ | FP32 sums +9.6 % and shared-expert fusion +0.7 % on `pascal-p106-v2`; persistent kernel bit-exact on a test GPU; multi-token kernels pass correctness | rig A/B of the persistent kernel and the multi-token kernels; rerun Q19's verify costs with them | rig |
| 7 | **Long context** | Q79, Q54, then Q59/Q60 | 32k fine (23.3 tok/s, 9 cards); the 64k drop is most likely llama-bench's saved state swapping the host | rig check without saved state; build the attention kernel only if attention is ≥ 30 % of a token at depth | rig |
| 8 | **Formats and the head** | Q92, Q112, Q113, Q114, Q115, Q90, Q91 ✓, Q56 ✓ | screened head exact offline (−0.153 GB per token, no quality cost); GPTQ Q5_0 head +0.002 KLD; Q6_K far gentler than Q5_0 (needs a Pascal 6-bit kernel); published GPTQ removes 0–13 % of imatrix rounding's cost, 68 % on Flint's head; F16 router saves 49 MB per token for free | Q113 F16 router into every candidate; Q112 GPTQ on three always-read groups (~3 GPU-hours); then Q114 experts, Q115 Q6_K; screened head in the fork (Q92); decide on a 6-bit kernel after the round-5 mixes | code; quant machine; rig |
| 9 | **Qwen3.6 track** | Q82, Q43 | Unsloth UD-Q4_K_XL KLD 0.013 sets its bar; MTP parked (1.04×) | rig speed of UD-Q4_K_XL on the fork | rig |
| 10 | **Owner decisions** | Q133, Q134, Q135, Q83, Q86 | memory offset est. +3–5 % decode at +10 % clock, tested exactly (KLD 0 when stable); PCIe 2.0 and power limits give nothing | the owner's OK for Q133 on GPU0 after the timed blocks (console within reach); Q134's package hold | rig, owner |

Parked: batched speculation plumbing (Q52, Q53) until Q78 lowers verify costs; Q37 (shared expert as routed experts),

retraining.

Every strategic decision, newest last: what was decided, on what evidence, and what it changed.

| Date | Decision | Evidence | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-23 | The quality bar is Unsloth's UD-Q4_K_XL (KLD 0.120 against BF16) | users run Unsloth's files; a ~9-point token-agreement gap is not worth speed | the makers' Q4_1 tier (S1, S2, makers-fast, Q4_0 variants) out of focus |
| 2026-10-23 | v2 work on the public repo's `v2` branch and on the fork's `pascal-p106-v2`; v1 (`main`, `pascal-p106`) stays as released | v1 released at `1a35d59` | five post-v1 commits stay on `main`; feature branches land on `pascal-p106-v2` |
| 2026-10-23 | Q41 runs the makers' way (T 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, ≥ 5 attempts, reasoning kept) | the makers' engine and cards (rec note) | chat benchmarks also switched to min-p 0 and kept reasoning (Q75) |
| 2026-10-23 | The makers' Q4_1 and makers-fast stay out of Q41 | Q74: their INT4 is plain round-to-nearest of BF16, so KLD judges it fairly | Q41 compares UD-Q4_K_XL with our own candidates only |
| 2026-10-23 | Batched speculation parked; EAGLE-3 training dropped | Q19 (a 3-token verify costs 2.2×), Q64 (n-gram ~1.03×), Q43 (MTP 1.04×) | Q20 dropped as a batched drafter; Q52/Q53 parked |
| 2026-10-23 | S4-L0 and S5 are no longer candidates; G3 is | Q73/Q77: with the fork's kernels Q5_0 costs ~1.1× Q4_1, so G3 (KLD 0.101) beats S5 for fewer bytes | Q41's main runs compare G3 with UD-Q4_K_XL |

## Registry

Status is **planned**, **running**, **done** or **dropped**. Quality numbers are same top token / mean KL divergence /
perplexity change against the reference named in the row, on 80 × 512 tokens of wikitext-2. Speed is real-chat tok/s on
the rig (llama.cpp `d77256999` + CUDA graphs, `-bs`, 262,144-token context) unless the row says otherwise.

Elixir models, training and model shrinking have their own repo,
[elixir-agents-on-mining-rigs](https://github.com/Cinderella-Man/elixir-agents-on-mining-rigs). Its registry,
`experiments.md`, keeps the original ids:
- the Elixir fine-tuning rows, Q472–Q492 and Q494–Q503;
- the DeepSeek-teacher rows, Q524–Q545;
- the open expert-pruning ideas: Q239, Q242, Q815, Q892, Q897, Q898, Q902, Q903, Q1141 and Q1142.

The finished expert-cut runs (Q232, Q890, Q891) stay here with their data.

New ideas of these kinds get their rows there, not here, and that registry is searched first, so no idea is found
twice:
- **Elixir and training:** fine-tuning, LoRA, distillation, RL, teacher models, task mining, Elixir models.
- **Model shrinking:** cutting or merging parts, such as expert pruning (REAP, RCO, usage cuts), expert merging (REAM,
  HC-SMoE), shared bases, layer or head drops, vocabulary trims, lottery tickets.

Run-time skipping that keeps the file whole stays here: the per-token expert skip (Q618), activation sparsity (Q622)
and the cold tier (Q238).

Category gaps (simple_python −3.7, parallel_multiple −7.5, parallel +5.5) are within one category's ~3-point noise at one attempt. **With attempt 3 on the first half (K = 2 on 1,738 tasks): G3 − UD −0.33 points, 90 % interval −1.24 to +0.58**, more room; full-pool sampling noise E[p(1−p)] 0.077, the first two attempts disagree on 15.5 %. **K2h passes single-turn non-inferiority on all 3,477 tasks (attempt 1): K2h 76.68 %, UD 76.53 %, K2h − UD +0.14 points, 90 % interval −0.87 to +1.16**; malformed 15 against 25 answers, runaway 7 against 6, a second `</think>` 18 against 32. **E1 decided for G3: non-inferior on all 3,477 single-turn tasks at K = 2:** UD 76.30 %, G3 76.65 %, G3 − UD +0.35 points over 6,954 matched samples, 90 % interval −0.40 to +1.09; malformed 0.75 % each, runaway 0.22 % against 0.16 %. Noise E[p(1−p)] 0.076 / 0.073, attempts disagree on 15.1 / 14.6 %. E2 noise (UD, 2 attempts on the pilot): E[p(1−p)] 0.100, self-discordance 20 %, so the 799-task lane needs K ≈ 2. **First LiveCodeBench pair (E3, 56 problems, K = 1, 32k budget, graded offline without network): UD 85.7 %, G3 80.4 %, G3 − UD −5.4 points (90 % interval −10.4 to −0.3) on only 3 discordant problems, all hard and all UD's (exact McNemar p = 0.25):** two where G3 hit 32,768 tokens with no code while UD finished at 32,144 and 23,005, one wrong answer at 32,763. Easy and medium 100 % for both, hard 71.4 vs 60.7 %, the 2025-02+ subset 88.2 % for both; G3 averages 13.3k tokens per sample against UD's 12.9k. At a 32k budget small length differences decide pass or fail, so E3 compares files only at 65,536 tokens. Cross-GPU pairing adds no noise (K2h–UD disagree on 13.9 %, G3–UD on the same GPUs on 14.6 %). K2h's second half, then K1h, running on GPUs 5–6. Shared seeds buy no variance reduction (UD a1 vs G3 a1 disagree on 14.3 %, a1 vs a3 on 14.4 %), so pairs may cross GPU sets. **First multi-turn pair (E2, 100 pilot tasks, K = 1): inconclusive, negative point estimate:** UD 39 %, G3 32 %, G3 − UD −7.0 points, 90 % interval −15.3 to +1.3 (SE 4.3, 5.0 clustered by scenario); the gap is mostly in miss_param (14 vs 8 of 25); malformed conversations 11 vs 14. E2's noise (verdicts differ on 19 % of tasks; E[p(1−p)] 0.118) needs K ≈ 2–3 on the 799-task lane; attempt 2 on the same 100 tasks running **New scenarios (49 tasks, disjoint from the pilot): G3 − UD 0.0 points (SE 8.3); combined 149 tasks at attempt 1: −4.7 points, SE 3.97 (4.34 clustered by scenario), 90 % interval −11.8 to +2.4, still inconclusive;** the pilot's −7.0 shrinks as scenarios are added; the format gate Δ +1.3 (upper +7.0) is too wide to call. The decision still needs all 799 tasks at K ≥ 2. **At 199 tasks: −5.0 points (SE 3.5, clustered 3.8; 90 % interval −11.2 to +1.2), discordant 30 against 20, McNemar p 0.20. One category replicates: miss_param UD 24, G3 14 of 47 (discordant 12 against 2, exact p 0.013, ≈ 0.05 after Bonferroni; pilot 14 vs 8 of 25, new scenarios 10 vs 6 of 22); G3 asks for the missing value as often as UD (44.7 % against 43.5 % of missing-value turns, `bench/bfcl_flint/ask_rate.py`); the deficit comes after the clarification: pass when it asked 38 % against UD's 75 %, when it acted 23 % against 35 %, and once given the value G3 more often ends with a wrong call result (6) or a wrong backend state (5); exploratory one-sided McNemar p = 0.0065.** base 25 vs 22, long_context 20 vs 19, miss_func 4 vs 8 (G3's favour) show nothing. **Pre-registered confirmatory test (139 fresh miss_param tasks, attempt 1, all pairs complete at 10:24): not supported; the exploratory signal was a false positive.** UD 34 (24.5 %), G3 46 (33.1 %); discordant 11 against 23; one-sided exact McNemar p = 0.988. G3 − UD +8.6 points (90 % interval +1.8 to +15.4); V100 set +15.9, GPUs 0–2 +1.4. In the turn missing a value UD asked 36.9 %, G3 33.6 %; pass when asked 44 against 47 %, when acted 12 against 26 %; failure types after asking alike (state mismatch 14 each). Exploratory and confirmatory together (descriptive): UD 58, G3 60 of 186. **Final E2 at attempt 1, 458 tasks: UD 150 (32.5 %), G3 154 (33.6 %), G3 − UD +0.87 points (SE 2.31, clustered 2.49; 90 % interval −3.23 to +4.98)**, 0.23 points short of the 3-point non-inferiority margin at K = 1; by category (UD/G3): base 44/41 of 89, long_context 37/32 of 92, miss_func 11/21 of 91, miss_param 58/60 of 186; without miss_param 92/94 of 272; format gate Δ +1.31 (upper +4.32). **New scenarios alone (358 tasks, the pilot's 100 left out): G3 − UD +3.07 points (90 % interval −1.59 to +7.73), inside the 3-point margin, but the format gate fails (Δ +1.40, upper +4.81).** **E1 decided: G3 non-inferior at K = 2 (+0.35, 90 % −0.40 to +1.09); K2h non-inferior at K = 1 (+0.14, 90 % −0.87 to +1.16).** E3's second pair was dropped for the miss_param test. Held jobs for a decision run are kept. Pilot (UD, 376 tasks × 5): pass@1 75.7 %, sampled attempts disagree on 17.8 % of tasks (greedy 11 %), so the full E1 pool needs 2 attempts. Per-task verdicts, ID lists and the pre-registration: [main runs](../docs/data/2026-10-24_q41/README.md), [pilot](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q41_pilot/README.md) | Protocol: S4-L0 and S5 against UD-Q4_K_XL (Q8_0 as anchor, BF16 on E1 only), sampled the makers' way ([rec note](../docs/research/notes/rec_engine.md): T 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, reasoning kept in the history, ≥ 5 attempts with pass@1 averaged; the makers call greedy decoding "not validated"). E1 BFCL v4 single-turn, 3,491 tasks, margin 1.5 points; E2 BFCL v4 multi-turn, 800 tasks, 3 points; E3 LiveCodeBench v6 (454 problems), 3 points. Pass if the lower 90 % bound of the paired difference is above −margin on every lane and malformed plus runaway outputs are at most 0.5 points above UD-Q4_K_XL's. | Q45 | On these cards Q5_0 costs nearly Q8_0's decode time per weight (1.32 against 1.50, Q4_1 0.95). Is Q8_0 in the sensitive layers plus Q4_1 in the rest better than Q5_0 everywhere? All on the S4-L0 recipe, quantized from BF16 | done | KLD / same top token / PPL against BF16, predicted tg128 (router F32): reference R0 = L0's recipe 0.123 / 88.5 % / +3.0 %, 23.4 tok/s (the composed L0 scored the same, so composing equals quantizing); **Q1 = R0 + attention q Q8_0 in layers 0–16: 0.111 / 88.9 % / +2.4 %, 23.0**, the best quality per ms found; G1 = gate/up Q8_0 in layers 2–24 and Q4_1 in 25–48: 0.122 / 88.5 % / +4.1 %, 23.8, but 36.3 GB; Q1D = Q1 + down-projections Q4_1 in 25–45: 0.123 / 88.1 % / +3.0 %, 23.4, 30.1 GB; Q1U = Q1 + gate/up Q4_1 in 25–48: 0.123 / 87.9 % / +3.1 %, 23.6; Q1DU = both: 0.131 / 87.3 % / +3.3 %, 24.0. G2 = gate/up Q8_0 only in layers 2–16, Q4_1 above: 0.131 / 87.9 % / +4.3 %, 24.1; X1 = shared expert Q4_1 in layers 25–48: 0.142 / 86.8 % / +6.6 % (the shared expert must stay Q8_0 everywhere); X2 = attention k/v Q4_1 in layers 25–48: 0.131 / 88.3 % / +4.3 % for 0.28 ms (keep Q8_0). Q2 = attention q Q8_0 in layers 0–24: **0.108 / 89.0 % / +3.1 %, 22.8** (the S5 recipe); GQ8 = gate/up Q8_0 in every layer instead of Q5_0: **0.103 / 90.1 % / +3.3 %** for ~1 ms (Q5_0 decodes almost as slowly as Q8_0), but ~42 GB. Final S5 file (Q2's recipe + F16 router, 32.6 GB): **0.105 / 89.1 % / +3.5 %**, predicted 23.0 tok/s. Near the bar every reallocation trades ~0.012–0.020 KLD per ms: the file-side frontier at UD-Q4_K_XL quality is ~23.5 tok/s (standard build) | quantizer recipes from BF16 with Unsloth's importance matrix, scored on one GPU set; [S5 recipe](recipes/flint-speed-s5.txt); every variant's recipe and score in [flint-mixes.tsv](recipes/flint-mixes.tsv) ([data](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q36_q45/README.md)) |
| Q46 | Can Pascal kernels for Q5_K and IQ4_XS (UD-Q4_K_XL's routed experts) reach the tuned Q4_1/Q8_0 kernels' bandwidth? If so, Unsloth's own file gets S4-L0's speed | done | Yes, both gates passed (Q5_K ≥ 110, IQ4_XS ≥ 90 GB/s): one super-block per lane, Q5_K's activation sums shared by all rows, IQ4_XS's table lookup with pre-permuted activations. Matrix-vector time per Flint token: Q5_K 31.6 → 13.9 ms (2.27×, 120 GB/s; 123 on the expert gate/up, 125 on the down-projection), IQ4_XS 34.9 → 13.7 ms (2.55×, 94 GB/s; 93, 102). IQ4_XS's fused gate/up needs one row per lane group (two rows: 255 registers, 40 GB/s). Staged through shared memory: Q5_K 128 GB/s (2.44×), IQ4_XS 104 (2.90× with direct loads for the fused gate/up). **Ported to the fork: UD-Q4_K_XL tg128 on 6 cards, one binary, kernels off → on: 24.49 → 34.06 tok/s (+39 %)**; 27.48 with only its Q8_0 part ported, 17.56 on stock llama.cpp (1.94×); prompt processing unchanged. Profiled GPU time per token: Q5_K 11.8 → 6.2 ms, IQ4_XS 7.1 → 3.3, Q8_0 20.5 → 14.6. Output (4 × 512 tokens, `-ub 1`) **unchanged within summation-order noise**: against the stock kernel with 2 warps KLD 0.053 / same top token 92.5 %, with 4 warps 0.062 / 92.3 %, while the two stock launch shapes differ by 0.060 / 92.6 %; perplexity 23.26 (Pascal kernels), 22.67 and 23.26 (stock) | [bench/mmvq_pascal](../bench/mmvq_pascal/) additions, 1–2 days, one card; [K-quant note](../docs/research/notes/kquant_decode_pascal.md); [data](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q46/README.md) |

| Q49 | With the Pascal kernels, does the 2-core host now limit the token rate? On S4-L0 the wall time per token fell less than the GPU time (Q38) | done (partly) | Not within a token: the host launches all 6 cards' graphs within ~1.9 ms of a token's start, while each card computes 4–8 ms (the copies between cards are asynchronous). Both event profilers put the kernels' saving at ~11.4 ms of GPU time per token (GPU chain 37.9 → 26.6 ms over the 6 cards), but llama-bench's token time falls 7.5 ms (34.25 → 26.76), and with the kernels off the unprofiled token is shorter than the profiled chain. CUDA events change the timing on these cards: 12 events per token slowed S4-L0 from 37.4 to 23.4 tok/s, with a ~27 ms stall on 18 of 47 tokens in both modes, so event timings compare kernels but don't give absolute token time. Open: a host-only timeline (no events) to see what the unprofiled run overlaps | fork's `GGML_CUDA_PROFILE_HOST` (host time per graph evaluation, GPU time from two events per evaluation, no synchronization), S4-L0 with the kernels on and off |

UD-Q4_K_XL on 6 cards, warmed llama-bench: tg128 33.99 → 34.24 tok/s, pp512 194.8 → 196.3. | Q51 | How many experts do consecutive Flint tokens share? Distinct experts per layer for 2, 4, 8 and 16 consecutive generated tokens against random routing (11.9 / 23.2 / 44.2 / 80.8). Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B shares about half between neighbours | done | **Little locality.** 2, 4, 8 and 16 consecutive generated tokens touch 11.1 / 20.1 / 35.6 / 59.7 distinct experts per layer (80 % of random at 8 tokens, against 51 % for Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B); adjacent tokens share 0.95 of 6. Whole-file edits route no differently (36.1 at 8). Layers 17–21 share the most (30–32 at 8 tokens), layers 2–6 the least (40–42). Renormalized route weights by rank 0.31 / 0.21 / 0.16 / 0.13 / 0.11 / 0.10; the sixth is below 5 % on only 6.4 % of token-layers, so skipping light experts gains nothing. Expert use is spread out: a layer's top 25 % of experts take 44 % of reads. So Q53's long block stays at 7 tokens, and a deduplicating multi-token `MUL_MAT_ID` kernel would save only 8–26 % of a 2–8-token verify's expert bytes | quant machine: Flint Q8_0 (sigmoid router, top-6 renormalized), 38 prompts (chat, code, whole-file edits, agent turns) generated at the makers' sampling (T 1.0) with exact token ids, 65,558 generated tokens; router top-6 and scores dumped for every position and layer ([bench/expert_overlap](../bench/expert_overlap/), [data](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q51/README.md)) |

Layer 1: cherry-pick upstream PR #31004 (graph key = first node plus its shape; keep the compute-6.1 gate and the fork's profiler key). Layer 2, only if rebuilds cost ≥ 2 %: per-shape graph results and schedulers in llama-context. Measure with A/B runs of `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` and `LLAMA_GRAPH_REUSE_DISABLE=1` and `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_DEBUG` reset counts. Worth est. +2–6 % with Q53's gate, +8–31 % for ungated drafters ([note](../docs/research/notes/speculation_plumbing.md)) |

| Q55 | What do tool requests lose without backend sampling? Every Flint tool request samples on the host for the whole request, on the fork and on master: the lazy tool grammar and the reasoning-budget sampler each switch `-bs` off when the sampler is created (`common/sampling.cpp:422-432`). Est. 3.2–3.5 ms per token (2.82 ms to copy 600,576 B of logits plus 0.4–0.7 ms on the host), (k + 1) × 2.82 ms per verify step; llama-bench's 26.8 ms already includes the copy | done | **Tool requests decode 12–16 % slower per token.** UD-Q4_K_XL on the combined build (Q108 off), real-chat server settings, the same tools request: on the long runs (2,700–3,000 tokens) backend sampling on gives 29.4–30.4 ms per token, sampling on the host (`backend_sampling: false`) 33.7–34.2 ms, and `tool_choice: auto` 33.3–34.0 ms, identical to host sampling (9 cards: 32.9 / 29.4 / 29.4 tok/s at T 0; 34.0 / 29.3 / 29.6 at T 1.0; 6 cards: 33.5 / 29.6 / 29.7 and 34.6 / 29.7 / 30.0). The server logs "backend sampling is not compatible with grammar, disabling" for every tools request. +3.6–4.8 ms per token, slightly above the 3.2–3.5 ms estimate; short replies (150 tokens) are too noisy to read. Decides Q67: worth +12–16 % on agent traffic | rig 1 h: one `-bs` server, the same tools request with `tool_choice` none, none plus `backend_sampling: false`, and auto; T 0 and T 1.0 with seeds. Decides Q67 ([note](../docs/research/notes/tool_request_sampling.md)) |

### Qwen3.6-35B-A3B

| ID | Question | Status | Result | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q29 | bartowski's Q4_0 of Qwen3.6-35B-A3B keeps its 248k-token output head (and attention q in four layers) in Q6_K: 22.7 % of each token's bytes, decoded at K-quant speed. How much faster is the same file with those tensors re-expressed as Q8_0? The MTP draft file carries its own Q6_K copy of the head | done (quality) | Lossless: KLD 0.037 / 92.0 % against bartowski's Q8_0, the same as the original (0.036 / 92.0 %). A lead, not checked: llama.cpp's radix top-k (`top-k.cu`) places the entries equal to the threshold in thread order (`atomicAdd`); passes of several tokens use it above 512 pools (1,024 for few rows), and one-token decode changes to it from a sort above 4,096 pools, which is 16,384 cells. First steps: the same request twice in one load (a race differs there, a load's state does not); prompts of 12,288, 16,384, 20,480 and 24,576 tokens for the threshold; the indexer's top-k forced to the sort ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-09_sparse_attn_whole_model/README.md)). **Q1175 (2026-11-10), the same build and line on agent-text prompts:** two loads of the plain line already differ at 8,192 prompt tokens (the first generated token's log-probability by 0.0236; the texts part at token 24) and at 16,384, 32,768 and 49,152; at 64 tokens every load of either line repeats to the last digit. So the threshold lies between 7,940 and 8,192 prompt tokens; 8,192 cells are 2,048 pools. The same request twice in one load: in one plain load the 8,128-token prompt read a second time from nothing gave all 256 log-probabilities of the reply exactly, in the next load 22 of 256; the draft line's two loads agree exactly at 8,192 and 16,384 and part at 32,768 and 49,152. The difference therefore comes and goes within a load as well as between loads ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-10_draft_line_exactness/README.md)). **Q1173 (2026-11-10), the release line with its sampler on whole agent requests** (66 requests of 12 sessions, prompts of 1,952 to 15,137 tokens, the same seed per request in every load): two loads of the preset wrote the same reply in 37 of 66 requests. The differences go by session: 7 of 12 sessions are the same in every request, 4 differ from their first request and 1 from its second, and after a session's first differing reply none of its 24 later replies is the same again, although every request's prompt is the recorded history and not the replay's own replies. A load's first request (2,780 prompt tokens read into an empty cache) wrote the same 73 tokens in three of four loads (the preset's two and the two with kept draws, a switch that never acts on this line) and 49 others in the fourth, below the 7,940 tokens at which three loads agreed on raw source text; the 6 requests from 8,192 prompt tokens are the same in both preset loads. [data](../docs/data/2026-11-03_flash_next_mtp/README.md). **Done in code (2026-11-04, C61).** [`scripts/ram_watchdog.sh`](../scripts/ram_watchdog.sh), embedded byte for byte in `bench/agent_check/rig_server.sh` and `scripts/server_bench.sh`, which start it with the server and refuse to leave it unguarded: MemAvailable minus CmaFree below 20 MiB for 2 s, memory pressure (some or full avg10) above 40 % for 10 s, more than 600 MiB swapped out in 10 s, and the old MemAvailable floor; `oom_score_adj` 1000 on the server and −1000 on itself; `chrt -f 10`; one TSV row a second (`ram_watchdog-<arm>.tsv`); every limit an environment variable (`WD_ENV` on the ssh command line). The defaults come from sixteen guard logs ([thresholds](../docs/data/2026-11-04_ram_watchdog/thresholds.tsv), [history](../docs/data/2026-11-04_ram_watchdog/history.tsv)): no run that finished reaches a limit, the pressure limit would have stopped Q558 step 3's arm 6.8 minutes after the server started, 16.5 before the kernel's kill, and the floor covers Q226's state (−18 MiB) but not that arm (131 MiB at its last sample). Self-test 64 of 64 on the quant machine (synthetic `/proc` files, a 60 s run on the real one, both embedding scripts with a stand-in server); not yet run on a rig. [data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_ram_watchdog/README.md) | code; bench/agent_check |

Inco AI's drafter is 0.6B (5 layers, blocks of 8, Apache-2.0, 334–652 MB as GGUF) and shares the target's embeddings and output head, so it adds est. 50–150 MiB of host memory against the Gyro MTP draft's ~1 GB; rollback keeps ~113 MiB of state per drafted token in VRAM. Est. 1.3–1.5× on the rig. **Q583 (2026-11-03) lowers it:** the 4-token verify costs 241.5 ms of GPU time, not 200–230, and a step ~55 ms more between the passes; with a 15–25 ms draft pass a DFlash2 step is est. ~337 ms against 127 ms per token, so est. 0.90× at 2.4 tokens per step, 1.07× at 2.83, 1.23× at 3.26: break-even at ~2.65 tokens per step (the MTP draft reaches 3.08). It needs Q591's kernels to gain much. Then a load-and-request smoke test, the agent check, and its use as Q550's guesser (Q554). **Done 2026-11-03 on the second rig ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-03_flash_next_dflash2/README.md)): DFlash2 generates faster than the MTP draft, but the host only holds it at `-b 512`, which slows the prompts.** With two drafted tokens and `-b 512` it passed 11 of 11 tasks. A drafted position costs ~87 ms a step (348 ms with three, 261 with two, against 120 ms a token without the drafter), so three don't pay at 2.84–3.11 tokens per step. Four crossed the watchdog's 150 MiB floor in the first prompt. Acceptance matches the published figures (3.11 tokens per step with three on the coding request, the MTP draft's 3.07–3.22), so Q572 is not needed. The first timed arm, at `-b 2048` (100 MiB of pinned layer-input buffer, four prompt micro-batches in flight), was stopped by the watchdog at the end of its first 2,776-token prompt. At p = 1 the logits equal the switch-off logits bit for bit (random qwen4exp, F32 and Q5_K heads) and Swift-1.5 Q2_0's text is the same in 12 of 12 requests. **Swift's router is not peaked:** on a CPU sample (~1,100 tokens × 48 layers) p = 0.9 keeps 8.62 of 10 experts (86 % of the routed reads; 9 in 66 %, 8 in 30 % of the pairs), p = 0.8 keeps 7.02 (70 %); both change the greedy text after a few words, which the KLD has to price. Bound on the gain: R13's 28 ms of routed experts per token × the share dropped, ~4 ms at p = 0.9 and ~8 ms at p = 0.8. [data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_bytes_switches/README.md). **KLD on FN1's bases (C122, so far):** `off` reproduces FN2's Swift Q2_0 (agent 0.2869, wiki 0.4289, code 0.1113); `p1` equals it on wiki and code and is 0.0002 lower on the agent text; p = 0.99 and w = 0.02 (renormalized) keep 10.000 and 9.994 of 10 experts and change no text's KLD beyond its paired noise (agent ±0.004, wiki and code ±0.0005): the 10th expert holds over 2 % of the weight in 99.8 % of the (token, layer) pairs, so only p ≤ 0.95 or w ≥ 0.05 can save reads. | Q682 | Mailbox hops between cards: card i's last kernel writes the residual (4 × 2,560 FP32, 40 KB) into mapped pinned host memory with a sequence flag, and card i + 1's first kernel polls it, so the host's synchronize, staged copy and launch leave each of the eight hops a token. The bytes need ≥ 0.4 ms a hop at the rig's ~200 MB/s (~3.4 ms a token); the profiled hops take 1.7–2.2 ms. Worth building if `bench/megakernel_p106 --hop` puts the unprofiled staged hop well above its bytes; saves est. (hop − 0.4 ms) × 8 a token. Reopened 2026-11-04 (the owner: implement it; C143) after S35: the Q8_0 cast through the staged hop took decode 14.98 → 17.06 tok/s on rig 1, −8.1 ms a token against ~2.6 ms of bytes, so the host's part of a hop counts | **code done 2026-11-04** (C143): built, CPU-checked and checked on two cards through the compute 6.1 PTX; the rig run (C143r) next | **C143** ([mailbox_hops](../docs/data/2026-11-04_mailbox_hops/README.md)): `GGML_CUDA_HOP_MAILBOX=1` (2 for pairs with peer access), off by default. In `cpy_tensor_async` a hop is one one-block kernel on each card, with no copy, event or host wait. The source card's kernel streams the tensor in chunks (`_CHUNKS`, 8) into a ring of `_SLOTS` × `_SLOT_KB` (16 × 32 KiB) slots in mapped pinned memory and raises a sequence-number flag after `__threadfence_system()`, refilling a slot only after its acknowledgement. The destination's kernel reads each chunk as it arrives (warp 0 checks the next 31 flags in one round trip) and acknowledges it. So the two PCIe links stream at once, which C140's one-shot mailbox (436 µs for 40 KB, the same as a host-staged copy) did not. Prompt-sized hops read on a copy stream behind the scheduler's guard (`_STREAM`); `_MAX_KB` sends them to the staged hop instead; a wait past `_TIMEOUT_MS` traps. On two RTX 3090s with the rig build's PTX: bytes exact in 9 ring settings (`test-hop-mailbox`, 27/27, the logged hop counts matching the copies taken); every mailbox arm gives the driver copy's logits bit for bit, and the Q8_0 cast through the mailbox gives S35's Q8_0 staged hop's bit for bit (`test-hop-cast`, 99/99 and 70/70). Kernels 18 / 21 registers, no local memory. Earlier, **C140** ([megakernel_rig](../docs/data/2026-11-04_megakernel_rig/README.md)): one token's F32 residual (40,960 B) takes 0.455 ms unprofiled with the driver's copy (3.6 ms a token over eight boundaries; the 1.7–2.2 ms was the profiler), bandwidth-bound (~26 µs fixed plus ~10 µs per KB); the one-shot mailbox takes 436 µs, the same as a host-staged copy (parked then). Fewer bytes and overlapped legs do better: the fork's staged hop (C201) 317 µs in 4 chunks, Q8_0 through it (C200) 127 µs | fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-mailbox` `5aebbddd9`; next C143r (rig 1: the hop bench, S35's line, server lines), then Q978, Q979; [megakernel.md](../docs/research/notes/megakernel.md) §4 |

| Q684 | (C417; the owner, 2026-11-04: implement it) A token-persistent pipeline: each card's persistent kernel stays resident across tokens, polls its input mailbox in mapped host memory (Q682), runs its layers and mails the residual on; the last card picks the token on the GPU and mails the id back to card 0, so the host leaves the decode loop. First its upper bound on the rig, measured with a stand-in workload before anything goes into the fork: `bench/persistent_pipe`, 2–9 cards each with four FP16 products over a dummy 10,240 × 10,240 matrix (est. ~5.6 ms a card on a P106), the persistent pipeline (a sequence flag per mailbox chunk, the residual written after the last product or while it runs) against the same work driven by the host (a synchronize per hop, the driver's peer copy, the fork's staged hop), the residual in F32 or Q8_0, 1 or 4 tokens a pass; every variant's ids and final residual bit-identical | **done 2026-11-05** (C417): the bound is measured; **the executor reopened 2026-11-05 (Q1024): planned as engine code (C538)**, since −0.77 ms a token from the hops alone (1.3 %) is a gain to stack once the persistent kernel's products reach the standalone kernel's speed (today they cost 3.58 ms a pass more, so with the clock held by a keep-alive it would lose ~2.8 ms; C538 does the products first). It was parked for size: it would reopen if C143r showed that llama.cpp's own host work on a hop is large and the mailbox hop (Q682) cannot remove it, or if no keep-alive holds the boost clock (Q1021) and the persistent kernel's products reach the standalone kernel's speed | **Rig run** ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_persistent_pipe/README.md#the-rig-run-c417-2026-11-05); nine P106s; every variant's ids and residual bit-identical). `persist-direct` against `staged` saves 5.69 ms a pass with Q8_0 hops (53.69 → 47.99 ms, −10.6 %), 7.15 ms with F32 hops (−12.9 %) and 30.76 ms at 4 tokens a pass (−23.8 %); on two cards it is 0.47 ms slower. **The hop matches the estimate:** 28 µs of a Q8_0 hop stays exposed against ~113 µs through the staged hop, and 60 against ~301 µs in F32. That saves 0.77 ms a token on Q8_0 hops (est. 0.9) and 2.2 ms on F32 hops (est. 2.5), which is 1.3 % of the release line's 58.6 ms token. **The rest is the clock.** The host-driven split left all nine cards at the 1,354 MHz base clock, and the persistent kernels held them at ~1,800–1,911 MHz. At base the products ran 19 % slower at 1 token (5.85 against 4.90 ms) and 37 % slower at 4 tokens, worth 8.5 and 34.3 ms a pass. Against that, the persistent kernel's own products ran 6–8 % slower than the standalone kernel's (3.6 ms a pass). Other readings: polls every 0.5–8 µs change a hop by ≤ 4 µs; a hop inside one switch takes 5–10 % longer than one into or out of card 4, which has its own root port; the persistent variants draw ~40 W a card against ~29. **Not worth building into the engine for its hops; the clock went to Q1021.** **Prototype** ([persistent_pipe](../docs/data/2026-11-04_persistent_pipe/README.md)): built for sm_61; the GPU smoke passed (two RTX 3090s, the Pascal PTX through the JIT and sm_86: 9 of 9 runs, every variant's ids and residual bit-identical, 2–4 stages, F32 and Q8_0, 1–8 tokens a pass). Est. from C140's hops (Q8_0 staged 127 µs, F32 357 µs; a persistent hop that writes while its last product runs leaves est. ~20 and ~60 µs exposed): −0.9 ms a token on the release line's Q8_0 hops, −2.5 ms with F32 hops, of a ~120 ms token, plus whatever of llama.cpp's own per-token host work an executor also removes (C405) | rig, 14 min (C417, done 2026-11-05); Q670's stages 1–3 and an executor (the floor note's option (b)) stay stopped with this bound; [megakernel.md](../docs/research/notes/megakernel.md) §4 |

The step itself is not measurably dearer (a fit over the requests: +0.51 ms at the mean depth, −0.39 to +0.96); the extra time is its 15 and 11 grammar rejections a load (the preset none), 389.65 ms each (174.61 to 771.03), all in replies that end in a tool call: Q1187's missing stop, est. +0.6 points when closed ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-10_prob_verify_rig/README.md)). Graph level rather than the scheduler (it copies every input of a split and cannot tell which may be rounded) or the CUDA copy (it would round every F32 copy between cards and exists only with two cards). On a CPU, with the switches off every architecture's logits equal the base's bit for bit (`test-llama-archs`, 136 of 136) and llama-server's speculative decoding gives the base's tokens and top-3 probabilities; the round trip F32 → F16 / BF16 / Q8_0 → F32 at [2560, 4, 1 / 4 / 512] is exact for data the type represents and within its rounding bound otherwise; with the round trip before layer 1 of a random qwen4exp only qwen4exp's logits change (max |Δlogit| F16 3.5e-5, BF16 2.6e-4, Q8_0 8.1e-4; error 2.1e-4, 1.7e-3, 5.4e-3 of the residual's rms), every hop within the bound, layer 0 unchanged; `test-llama-context` 19 / 0 failed with it on. **On the rig (C200r, rig 2, 2026-11-04):** pmix-q2-fast on `-ladder3` with SMEM=32768, S12's no-draft line, `LLAMA_HOP_CAST=q8_0` against f32 hops: decode +10–15 % (14.5 → 16.2, 16.2 → 18.3, 13.7 → 15.8, 9.2 → 10.1 tok/s at short, ~1k, ~8k, ~32k prompts), prompts +22–31 % (61 → 75, 141 → 184, 155 → 189 tok/s); with C200k's KLD pass it goes on the release line. **A second load the next morning (C200r, 2026-11-05)** gives the same tokens, decode within 0.3–1.3 % at short, ~1k and ~8k and prompts within 0.2–1.6 % of the first (the spread): **gain**, decode +11–15 % and prompts +21–32 % on both loads; F16 (Q231) is slower than Q8_0 on every request the spread lets us read, and the staged hop adds nothing measurable on top (Q751: negligible). Greedy arms: mismatches (not the scorer's argmax) and large-margin events against a no-draft arm of the same build; rejected at a mismatch rate more than 0.5 points above the no-draft arm's at 95 %, or at more unexplained large-margin events. Sampled arms (the agent check's temperature 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, and the grammar where one applied): tokens outside the sampler's support, and a χ² test of the randomized probability integral transform u = F(x⁻) + V·p(x) against the no-draft arm's. It finds what task pass rates cannot (SpecPilot's injected logit bias moved accuracy by −1.6 points, inside the noise, and failed token forcing at 2.0–2.4 % mismatches), and Q808-type biases on real sessions. | Q1021 | (C532, C533; from Q684's rig run and Q154) Hold every card at its boost clock in the layer split with a keep-alive kernel, without a persistent pipeline. On nine cards each card idles ~8/9 of a token, and the driver keeps it at the 1,354 MHz base clock; any resident kernel holds it at ~1,800–1,911 MHz. In Q684's run the stand-in's FP16 products ran 19 % slower at base at 1 token and 37 % slower at 4 tokens. The keep-alive is one small block per card on a low-priority stream of its own. It polls a flag in mapped host memory with a back-off, starts when decoding starts, and is stopped by the flag before any device-wide synchronize or free, because a resident kernel would block them. A short spin kernel relaunched while a card idles is the alternative. Steps: (1) a 100 ms clock log per card while the release line decodes and through one agent turn, to see whether Flash-Next's cards sit at base (Q154 found Flint at boost 81 % of the time and Ornith at base 50 %); (2) `bench/persistent_pipe --keepalive` on the host-driven variants, to see whether one block holds the boost, what it costs the products on 5 SMs and what it draws; (3) the fork behind an environment variable, on T(k) and the agent check. Lossless | **step (1) done 2026-11-05 (C532): the cards sit at base; a gain, est. 5–9 ms a token on the served decode**; steps (2)–(3): **code done 2026-11-05 (C533)**; **the rig run done 2026-11-05 (C533, rig 2): `decode` a gain**, tg128 −8.5 to −11.7 ms a token (−14 to −19 %); **agent check done 2026-11-05 (C533a, rig 2): `decode` a gain with the MTP draft and goes into the release preset**: generation 20.75 against 19.19 tok/s (+8.1 %, 48.2 against 52.1 ms a token), prompts +12.5 %, −8.4 % a task at equal work, 12 / 12, acceptance unchanged ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-05_keepalive/README.md#the-agent-check-the-three-settings-with-the-mtp-draft-c533-c558)) | **C533 rig run** ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-05_keepalive/README.md#the-result-on-a-rig-c533-2026-11-05); a 4 GB rig, the release line without the draft, the `-keepalive` build off and `decode`, two loads each, alternated): tg128 62.75 and 59.62 ms a token off against 51.06 and 51.08 on (15.9–16.8 → 19.6 tok/s), T(4) 131.5 and 124.1 → 101.2 and 101.1 ms, T(5) 153.9 and 146.3 → 118.2 and 118.3 (−19 to −23 %), T(3) −13 to −22 %, T(2) −8 to −16 %; T(1) unchanged: as llama-bench's first test it runs while the cards still ramp up (Q1077). While tg128 runs every card sits at 1,354 MHz off and at 1,873–1,911 MHz on, 27–30 W a card off against 35–40 W (+72 W for the nine, +5 to +11 % energy a token). The smoke logged a line per card; none of the 117 block reports ended by its timeout. **Class: gain** (the off loads differ by 3.1 ms a token, the on loads by 0.02). Above C532's estimate: est. ~7 ms a token on plain decoding; scaled to the served MTP decode 7.7–10.2 ms a token (est. 5.5–9). The bench on nine cards, Q8_0 staged hop: off 53.75 and 52.28 ms a pass, resident 45.88 (×3), pulse 45.13 (×3); at 4 tokens 115.1–121.2, 93.91, 93.48; both hold every card at 1,875 MHz, the pulse 0.75 ms a pass cheaper (Q1065). Under CUDA 12's lazy module loading a resident kernel blocks a first kernel launch on its card until it ends: the first bench run's resident passes ran after the threads' own timeouts, so `run_rig.sh` loads every kernel at start (Q1075 for the engine). `request` and `idle` (Q1045) not measured: llama-bench takes no requests. **C533 code** ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-05_keepalive/README.md)): `bench/persistent_pipe --keepalive resident\|pulse` (one thread a card on a stream of its lowest priority, around the calibration and every host-driven variant, measuring the SM clock; the calibration with the thread and right after it stopped) and Miner Llama's `GGML_CUDA_KEEPALIVE=decode\|request\|idle` (`_MS`, `_POLL_US`; `-keepalive` `f6c1c6a4a` on `-ladder4`, off by default): one block of one thread per card polls a word in mapped host memory every 50 µs, starts after each graph on its card, is ended before a capture on the card and before frees, pinned host allocations and device-wide synchronizes, and ends itself `_MS` after the card's last graph (1 s; 120 s for `idle`); each block logs the SM clock it measured. CPU checks passed (build machine, no GPU): the rig and CPU builds give -ladder4's logits on every architecture `test-llama-archs` builds, with the switch off and with `decode` set; `test-cuda-keepalive` 86/86 in 3 runs. Rig tarball md5 `d18fccc9…`. **C532** ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-05_card_clocks/README.md); rig 1, S35's line, a 100 ms log per card): plain decoding leaves every card at the 1,354 MHz base clock in every sample, at 1 token a pass (tg128, 16.16 tok/s) and at 4 (T(4) 128.7 ms), in P0 with the memory at its full 4,006 MHz and no clock-event reason set. The served first agent turn: the prompt at boost 77 % of the time; the MTP decode 55 % base, 34 % boost, but its boost is the prompt's carried over: 4–7 s into the decode cards 0–3 and 5–7 (41 of the 49 layer shares) fall to base and stay there, while card 4 (the draft's token embeddings) holds 1,797 MHz and card 8 (the draft head) 1,885. **Class: gain (est.)**: with every card at boost, est. ~7 ms a token on plain decoding (−12 %) and 5.5–9 ms a token on the served decode (14–23 ms a 4-token pass, −8 to −14 %), from the stand-in's 16 % (1 token) and 27 % (4 tokens) and Flint's 12 % (Q154); cost est. +10 W a decoding card. Boost lasts 4–7 s of light load before a card drops, and a card out of P8 ramps within ~1 s; new Q1045, Q1046. Before: Q684's rig run: the host-driven variants on nine cards at 1,354 MHz in every sample, the persistent ones at ~1,800–1,911 MHz; ~29 against ~40 W a card ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_persistent_pipe/README.md#where-the-saving-comes-from-the-clock-not-the-hop)) | (1) rig, 13 min (C532, done 2026-11-05); (2) code, then ~5 min on a rig (C533). Est. 5–9 ms a token on the served decode after C532 (2–5 before it, the [floor note](../docs/research/notes/flash_next_floor_and_engine.md)) |

The two loads with the draft repeat the first run's `sparse` loads to within 1.60 ms a step on the repeated request up to 32,768 and 4.14 ms at 49,152. Q94 dropped the bias on single requests (Flint: 2.4 reflection words per 1,000 reasoning tokens), but in the agent check's v3 release arms Flint has 10.41 per 1,000 (4,213 in 404,874 reasoning tokens: "hmm" 1,674, "wait" 1,506, "actually" 831) and Ornith 8.94 (770 in 86,124), both above the ~5 line, with 9.09 % and 6.71 % of the reasoning in sentences that such a word opens; reasoning is 72.88 % and 47.42 % of their task time. NoWait's method (arXiv 2506.08343): a bias on the list's word-initial tokens while the model reasons. llama-server's `logit_bias` acts on the whole reply, so a bias limited to the reasoning is a sampler switch. Paired arms of the agent check: tokens and minutes per solved task, and the pass rate, since the bias changes the text; a gain becomes a preset variant. A 4-token verify takes other kernels than one-token decoding, so rounding can turn a near-tie; a fault in the speculative path would show the same way. Settle it per divergence point: the two candidates' probabilities and the lead at the first differing token, read from the plain line teacher-forced on the common prefix; then every token of both arms' replies against that teacher-forced distribution (tokens that are not its first choice, and the lead they lose by). Leads within rounding at every point → the draft line writes the model's own text to rounding, and the number goes on the model page; a lead beyond rounding anywhere → the speculative path is searched (rollback of the recurrent state first) before any other draft work | done 2026-11-10 (FN44) | **Not the card split, not a near-tie, and not the accept step or its rollback: a pass over two or more tokens is another arithmetic than a one-token pass, and this model turns that into tenths of a log-probability within a few tokens.** With one explicit split in both arms (every layer on the same card) the replies still part at all five depths, at tokens 7, 2, 17, 26 and 43. There the plain line's token leads by 0.2100, 1.7798, 0.1516, 0.0692 and 0.1443 in its own request and the draft line's token by 0.2032, 0.7537, 0.2965, 0.1086 and 0.1581 in its request: no lead is within 0.05, and each line wrote the first choice of its own distribution at every token. At depth 0, where every load of either line repeats to the last digit, the two lines agree exactly on the first token and differ by 0.0138 on the next, the first one a verify pass computes; the plain line reading the seven common tokens back as a prompt prefers the draft line's token by 0.6213; teacher-forced one token a pass it repeats its own 256 log-probabilities exactly, and 5 of the draft line's 249 later tokens are not its first choice (by 0.0382 to 0.3781; KL divergence over the listed tokens 0.0055). A draft of one token and a draft of three write the same 256 tokens with the same log-probabilities to the last digit (135 steps with 15 rejections against 84 steps, 38 of them not kept whole), which clears the accept step and the rollback: verify passes of two and of four tokens are one arithmetic, the one-token pass another. From 8,192 tokens on the text depends on the load, not on the draft: two loads of the plain line part after 24, 17, 26 and 141 tokens, the seven loads of both lines (Q1172's two among them) wrote 6, 5, 7 and 7 different texts, the draft at one token wrote Q1172's plain text at 16,384, and the plain line teacher-forced on its own reply misses 17 of 1,024 first choices against 21 of 936 on the draft line's (Q575). So the draft line keeps the main model's first choice in the pass that verified it, and not the plain line's text; a model page should say the first. The run decoded with llama.cpp's own kernels (Q1178). New rows Q1179 and Q1180. | Q1177 | (from Q1171, 2026-11-10) What turns a rounding difference into 0.1 of log-probability within one token on Flash-Next, and a whole-model check that sees a 1 % fault. In Q1171 the sparse attention kernel with its lists in 1, 10 or 32 splits (the same sums in another order) moved the next token's log-probabilities by 0.0945, 0.109 and 0.11 at 7,940 tokens, where every arm repeats to the last digit; a deliberate 1 % error in the kernel's output moved them by 0.139. FP32 rounding is about 10⁻⁷ of a value, so a stage after the attention multiplies it some 10⁵ times. The candidates are the discrete stages: the QSA indexer's cut at 512 pools of ~2,000 (a pool that changes sides swaps 4 attended cells), the experts' top-k behind the F16 router, and the Q8_0 cast at each card boundary. Method, off the rig: two split counts as the pair of arms; the next-token difference again with one stage at a time switched off or widened (`LLAMA_HOP_CAST` unset, one card, the router in F32); and the indexer's picks per layer counted between the two arms (needs a small dump switch). Outcome: the stage (if it is the selection, a margin at the cut is an idea of its own), and the yardstick every greedy comparison on this model needs (Q1175, Q1143's arms): the spread between split counts, against which a difference between two arms is judged; equal hashes stay evidence, unequal ones are not a fault | planned 2026-11-10 | — | a machine with three 24 GB cards; engine; after Q575's first steps; [Q1171's data](../docs/data/2026-11-09_sparse_attn_whole_model/README.md) |

A pass makes every later off-rig run of the release line the rig's own kernels to rounding | planned 2026-11-10 | — | a machine with three 24 GB cards; [switches](../docs/switches.md); [Q1171's data](../docs/data/2026-11-09_sparse_attn_whole_model/README.md) |
| Q1179 | (from Q1175, 2026-11-10) Which operation computes one token another way than two or more, and a verify pass that repeats one-token decoding bit for bit. In Q1175, at depth 0, where every load repeats to the last digit, the draft line's first verify pass gives the next token a log-probability 0.0138 away from the plain line's one-token pass over the same token on the same state; verify passes of two and of four tokens agree in all 256 log-probabilities; and the plain line reading seven of its own tokens back in passes of several turns a lead of 0.2100 into −0.6213. So the one-token pass is the odd one out. Candidates, by reading: llama.cpp's matrix-vector kernels take another reduction shape for one column than for two to four, the experts' products have a one-token path, and the attention's indexer sorts for one token where wider passes can take the radix top-k (Q575's lead); on the rig the k-column kernels stand against the one-token Pascal kernels the same way (Q1178). Method, off the rig, at 64 to 320 tokens where nothing else varies: the next token's log-probabilities after the same tokens decoded one a pass and two a pass; then each candidate operation put on the other shape's kernel in turn (a switch per operation), and `llama-eval-callback` dumps for the first tensor that differs. Outcome: the operation. If its two shapes can share one order of sums, a verify pass repeats one-token decoding bit for bit, the draft line writes the plain line's text, and "the same hash with and without the draft" becomes a release test at depths where loads repeat | planned 2026-11-10 | — | a machine with one 24 GB card or more; engine; beside Q1177 (the same switches); [Q1175's data](../docs/data/2026-11-10_draft_line_exactness/README.md) |
| Q1180 | (from Q1175, 2026-11-10) Is a pass over several tokens worse than one-token passes, or only different? Q1175 measures the distance between the two (at depth 0, 2.0 % of the draft line's tokens are not the plain line's first choice; KL divergence over the listed tokens 0.0055) but not which is nearer to the model. The reference: the same file with FP32 sums on a CPU. Against it, the KL divergence and the share of equal top tokens of passes of 1, 2, 4 and 512 tokens (`llama-perplexity --kl-divergence` with `-b` and `-ub` set to the width) on the three texts (agent, code, wiki), chunks of 512 tokens, where loads repeat; a second load of the one-token arm gives the floor. Wider passes at the one-token arm's distance → the draft and the prompt's passes cost no quality, only another draw of the same model; farther → every draft step pays, and Q1179's fix is worth its price | planned 2026-11-10 | — | a machine with one 24 GB card or more, and the CPU reference; est. 1–2 h on the cards |
| Q1181 | (from Q928's GPU checks, 2026-11-10; C670) The one-token expert product of N2P-128 and N3P-128 at an odd row count. On `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-n2p` `1a0832375`, test-backend-ops' case `MUL_MAT_ID(type_a=n2p_128\|n3p_128,type_b=f32,n_mats=32,n_used=10,b=0,m=2561,n=1,k=640)` fails in every run that holds it, and no other case fails. N3P-128 with the fork's kernels through the compute 6.1 PTX: error 0.000762, 0.000563, 0.000805, 0.000697 and 1.000003 in five runs (the limit is 0.0005). Both types on llama.cpp's matrix-vector kernel: 0.000518 and 1.000000 through the PTX, 0.989390 and 0.001466 natively on compute 8.6. The same matrices pass without expert ids, with 2–8 tokens, and at 2,560 rows. An error that moves between runs of one kernel and shows on two kernels points away from one kernel's arithmetic, towards a read outside a row or an expert (an expert of 2,561 rows of 220 or 270 bytes does not end at a 16-byte boundary; one of 2,560 rows does), the CPU reference, or the test's own data. Find which: the failing case alone, with a fixed seed and each side's output dumped; then fix it and rerun `gpu_checks.sh`. No file has such a row count, so nothing measured depends on it, but the types should not ship with an open failure | **answered** 2026-11-10 (C671): the release build has the fix; left: the case natively on a P106 (C394's replay holds it) | **The v3.0 release build `1551f5fe1` does not fail it, and its last commit is the fix.** Run live through the compute 6.1 PTX, the case passes 35 times of 35 on `1551f5fe1` (20 with the fork's Pascal kernels on, 15 with them skipped; an RTX 3090 and an RTX 3080) and fails 13 times of 20 on `1a0832375` on the same RTX 3080 the same day (N3P-128 5 of 5 with the Pascal kernels on, errors 0.000787041 to 0.999998177; 8 of 10 with them skipped, one a NaN at output index 2563). Cause: llama.cpp's `mul_mat_vec_q` bounded its rows by the output's stride, which for a one-token expert product is the row count times the experts used; with a row count that is not a multiple of the block's rows, each expert's last block wrote past its own rows over the next expert's first rows, racing with that expert's own block. `1551f5fe1` bounds the rows by the matrix's row count, and its message names this case. The `-n2p` branch's own build predates it, which is where the GPU checks ran ([the check set for a P106](../docs/data/2026-11-04_n2p_flash/README.md#the-check-set-for-a-p106), [case_m2561_live.tsv](../docs/data/2026-11-04_n2p_flash/case_m2561_live.tsv)) | a machine with one CUDA card (test-backend-ops, then code); [the failing runs](../docs/data/2026-11-04_n2p_flash/gpu_check_failures.tsv) |

No engine code: a proxy in front of N llama-servers. Est. from Q1184’s 240 replies (12 second replies of bug-fix tasks, ten samples an arm): the first of 2 samples generates 0.621 of one sample’s tokens, of 3 0.498, of 4 0.436, of 5 0.399 (the preset; 0.593, 0.472, 0.417 and 0.384 with probabilistic verification). In slots of one rig the same race costs est. 0.95 (2 samples) and 0.87 (4) of one sample’s time, since a 2-token pass takes 77.9 and 78.4 ms and a 4-token pass 101.6 and 106.3 ms against 51.1 and 52.3 ms a token alone: the race pays across rigs, not inside one. Open: whether the first finisher passes as many tasks (Q93’s offline gate on another model found no loss), the share on whole sessions, and what the losers’ read of the winner’s reply costs | planned; 2026-11-10: published races are on mathematics and science questions only (the first of four samples generates 0.74, 0.68, 0.79 and 0.76 of one sample's tokens on four models, arXiv 2505.18149); a late start for the second sample is Q1199 ([note](../docs/research/notes/tokens_per_solved_task.md), section 5) | Priced from published rows: [race_price.md](../docs/data/2026-11-10_prob_verify_reply_length/race_price.md). | Q1201 | (same note; adds to Q1166) Earlier replies' reasoning removed by the harness, in blocks. The model's chat template keeps the reasoning of every assistant message after the last user message whatever `preserve_thinking` says, so `--no-reasoning-preserve` drops nothing inside an attempt of one user message: check it by rendering one recorded request both ways (`/apply-template`). Removing it takes a pi `context` handler that strips `reasoning_content` from replies older than the last K. Done every turn, each call restores a checkpoint and reads the reply's answer again (est. 1.3 s); done once per block of turns, one re-read per block. Against it: the makers ask to keep the reasoning, and OpenAI reports about +3 % on SWE-bench for keeping it between tool calls. Published (The Complexity Trap, arXiv 2508.21433, SWE-agent, 500 SWE-bench Verified cases, M = 10): cost per case −52.7 % at 54.8 % solved against 53.4 % (Qwen3-Coder 480B), −9.8 % at 24.6 % against 23.0 % (Qwen3-32B with thinking), and −57.1 % at 36.4 % against 40.4 % (Gemini 2.5 Flash with thinking). On the rig a cached prefix is free and an edit of history is read again at prompt speed, so a mask that slides every turn would re-read the window every turn; applied when the context passes a mark, it costs one re-read per block (est. 4.8 min for 20,000 kept tokens at 69.4 tok/s) for a shallower cache afterwards and an attempt that fits the window. A pi `context` handler. First a count on recorded real attempts: the share of context tokens that are tool results older than 10 turns; then a replay arm for the time, and a bench arm of the sister repo for the tasks | planned 2026-11-10 | — | counts on a CPU, then a rig replay arm |
| Q1203 | (same note) pi's summary compaction as a token lever. It triggers at the window minus `reserveTokens` (16,384), keeps the last `keepRecentTokens` (20,000) and asks the model to summarise the rest. At a 65,536-token window that is est. 12 min a compaction on the release line (est. 7 min to read the old history, est. 4.8 min to read the kept part again, plus the summary's own tokens), and summaries lengthened runs by 15 % at similar solve rates in The Complexity Trap | planned, **parked** 2026-11-10: the published effect is longer runs and the rig pays each compaction in prompt time | — | reopens for attempts that do not fit the window after Q1202's mask; then `session_before_compact` with a summary built without a model call |
| Q1204 | (same note; adds to Q268, which had no answer to converge on in tool-call turns) Early exit by a tool-call probe. The answer of an agent turn is its next tool call, 49.8 tokens on average in Q1184's second replies. A probe every N reasoning tokens samples the call that would follow now; when consecutive probes agree, the reasoning is ended through the server's control endpoint, which the release tree has (`reasoning_control: true` in the request, then `POST /v1/chat/completions/control` with `action: reasoning_end`). A probe that lets the reasoning go on needs a copy of the sequence's state, the recurrent state included: engine code, or a second slot fed the same prefix. Published on single answers only: 11 to 29 % fewer tokens at equal accuracy on mathematics (Dynasor, arXiv 2412.20993); on code 8,203 → 5,437 tokens at 73.4 → 74.1 for Qwen3-14B on LiveCodeBench (DEER, arXiv 2504.15895). Q1197's curve is the ceiling: the call fixed at half of the reasoning in under 30 % of replies → parked | planned 2026-11-10 (after Q1197) | — | Q1197 first; then a proxy and the state copy |

| Q1192 | (C678, from Q1191, 2026-11-10) A request's `ubatch_taper` on a line with an MTP draft. A recurrent model's trailing `n_rs_seq` + 1 tokens stay in one micro-batch (the rollback snapshots), and `split_equal` raises every micro-batch of a planned split to `n_rs_seq` + 2 tokens: with a draft of three, five. So C271's T(k) requests cannot give passes of one to four tokens on the preset's own line, and Q1191 profiled the line without the draft. With `GGML_CUDA_KEEPALIVE=decode` a card's block runs for 1 s after each of its graphs, so a kernel first launched within that second waits up to the block's 1 s timeout, and that block's report says "ended by the timeout". llama-bench showed none (117 block reports, every one ended by the host: each test's warm-up loads its kernels before its first block); llama-server may (a prompt's kernels right after a decode, a new batch shape, the draft's first pass). | Q929 | (the owner, 2026-11-04: "maybe we can make a format even more friendly to a Pascal machine"; C369) A Pascal trellis code. Gyro-M shows a trellis code at ~2.1 bits holding half Q2_0's KLD at Q2_0's size (0.148 against 0.285), but its decode costs 10–38 ps a weight on a P106 (C335: a 32-bit hash multiply, which Pascal splits into 16-bit XMADs, and FP16 codebook reads). Design a code that decodes in Pascal's cheap operations: the state's top bits index a small codebook directly (QTIP's LUT form, no multiply), entries stored as int8 4-vectors in shared memory so the product is one dp4a per 4 weights per column, 128-weight blocks with one FP16 scale, optionally a Hadamard rotation (it hurt scalar codes in Q304 but trellis codes rely on it). Est. ~2–3 instructions per 4 weights plus one dp4a per column, near Q2_0's 3.3 ps. Step 1 is a CPU proxy, importance-weighted error on Swift's expert weights against Q2_0, N2P, IQ3_XXS and Gyro's TQ2_T (reference encoder), at 2.0–2.6 bits; then a P106 decode microbench; only then the encoder, the file and the kernels | step 1 done 2026-11-04 (C369, CPU proxy); step 2 is Q955 | **CPU proxy on 84 Swift-1.5 expert matrices** (layers 2–47, gate/up/down; FN4's importance matrix; importance-weighted error, Swift's own Q2_0 bytes = 1.0): TQ2_T from our own Viterbi encoder 0.442 at 2.125 bits (Gyro-M's own bytes 0.601, the checkpoint difference ~2 % of that). **HP4S, a trellis code decoded in registers** (16-bit state, 8 bits a step: one 16 × 16-bit XMAD hashes the state, four 3-bit fields pick each of 4 weights from one of two 8-level int8 tables held in registers with one `PRMT`; no shared memory, no 32-bit multiply): **0.459, +3.9 % of TQ2_T's error, est. 4.6 ps a weight on a P106** (TQ2_T 10.0 measured, Q2_0 3.3); `hp4.n16` the same quality at est. 4.8, `hp4.n8` +17 % at 4.0, Gyro's 32-bit hash no better than one XMAD. Codebooks in shared memory reach TQ2_T's error (`lut2` ±0, `hash4` +0.4 %) but each random read per 4 weights costs ~3 ps a weight (est. 6.4–10, Q957); TCQ in registers +25 to +52 % (Q958); small per-lane codebooks 2–2.8× (Q959). The 128-wide Hadamard rotation hurts every trellis code (+10 to +18 %), so no run-time rotation; int8 entries cost nothing. At 2.625 bits `hp4.n16.k25` 0.249, below N2P-128 at 2.75 bits (0.324; Q956). The decode estimate (issue slots plus random shared-memory reads, fitted to Q2_0) is within ±12 % of the measured N2P, IQ2_S, IQ3_XXS and TQ2_T kernels. **Verdict: yes**; step 2 (Q955, C400) times HP4S on a P106 → [data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_pascal_trellis_proxy/README.md) | quant machine (CPU proxy), rig (microbench), fork |

| Q978 | (C430, from Q682) The mailbox hop inside the cards' CUDA graphs: the writer as the last node of the source card's graph and the reader as the first node of the destination's, so a hop costs the host no launch at all. Q682's hop is two kernel launches by the host per hop, outside the graphs, because the scheduler copies a split's inputs between graph evaluations. The graph needs to know which of its outputs cross a boundary and into which slot, so the scheduler would hand the CUDA backend the boundary's copies at graph build (an output-to-mailbox map per split). Saves two launches per hop (est. 10–30 µs each on the rig's Celeron) and lets a card's next graph start without the host. Lossless | planned | — | fork (scheduler, CUDA backend), after C143r shows the mailbox's host part |
| Q979 | (C431, from Q682) What the mailbox's waiting cards cost: while a token runs on card i, the readers of cards i + 1 … 8 already spin one block each and poll their links at most every `GGML_CUDA_HOP_MAILBOX_POLL_US`, so the cards neither idle nor drop their clocks. Measure board power and temperature per card over a decode run with the mailbox on and off; if the cost is large, wait without an SM: the stream wait-value operation on the mapped flag (Q752), or a longer poll pause on cards far from their turn | planned | — | rig (power), fork |

| Q900 | (C338, the owner, 2026-11-04: "can we make it faster?") The verify pass's logits and host sampling. A k-token verify needs a logits row per drafted position: 248,320 F32 values, ~1 MB a row, so ~5 MB for k = 5 from the last card over its PCIe 1.0 x1 link (est. ~20 ms at ~250 MB/s), then sampling and the acceptance test on the 2-core host (top-k/top-p, the tool grammar, est. several ms a row). `llama-bench` never pays it (one output row), so T(k) misses it, while every agent-check verify does. Step 1, measure: the light profiler and the server's timings on a real MTP agent-check verify (S34's draft arm), D2H bytes and host time per pass. Step 2, if large: backend sampling for verify batches: the k rows sampled and the drafts' acceptance tested on the last card, returning the accepted ids, the residual sample and the few probabilities the rule needs; the host fetches a full row only where the tool grammar rejects the card's pick (Q67's fallback). Exact for greedy and for token-by-token rejection with the same draws | done 2026-11-04: step 1 measured (C338, rig 2); step 2 closed without code, since `-bs` already does it (source reading) | **Measured on S34's MTP line** (pmix-q2-fast, `-ladder3`, SMEM=32768, one request, 186 steps, `GGML_CUDA_PROFILE_HOST`): a step is 179 ms (median), of which the verify pass's GPU time over the nine cards 113.6 ms, the draft's 24.8 ms, and **~41 ms (23 %) on no card's clock**: the eight hops (est. ~9 ms), the four draft passes' host turnarounds, sampling and bookkeeping; the host profile cannot split it ([data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_verify_host/README.md)). **Step 2 needs no code: with `-bs`, on every agent-check line, the verify batch is already sampled on the last card** by the request's own chain (one chain per verified row, `n_outputs_max_per_seq` = draft length + 1; `needs_raw_logits` false, so `t_logits` stays on card 8). Per row only the sampled id and the top-k 20 candidates' logits, probabilities and ids come back (244 B, ~1 KB a pass against 3.97 MB for four full rows), and token-by-token rejection runs on them. A full row is fetched only after a tool-grammar rejection (Q67); the MTP draft's rows go through a device top-k 10 chain (`--spec-draft-backend-sampling`, on by default); every op of the chain is supported on CUDA with no compute-capability condition ([device_verify](../docs/data/2026-11-04_device_verify/README.md), fork `-kshape` `d27845b67`, the same code as `-ladder3`). The ~41 ms is hops and host turnarounds: its split is Q960, the draft passes' turnarounds Q961. Estimate from C271 and C140 (rig1-runner, 2026-11-04): a logits row is 248,320 × 4 B ≈ 1 MB, est. ~5 ms a row to the host over PCIe 1.0 x1 (C140's host-staged copies: ~5 µs per KB a leg), plus the output layer on k rows (Q5_K: 3.1 ms at one row on the one-token kernel, ~7–9 ms at 2–5 rows on the k-column kernel with the 32 KB budget); llama-bench's T(k) holds one row and C271's real-text T(k) none ([kreal_p106](../docs/data/2026-11-04_kreal_p106/README.md)) | rig (measure), fork (code) |

In the long request, each load's first, the two loads with the cache wait on the host in 46 of 90 and 16 of 29 steps (median 115.9 and 120.5 ms, between draft passes) while the host swaps out 337 and 64 MiB (1 to 29 MiB in the four loads without it); the cause is open (Q1209). Cost files for `LLAMA_SPEC_ADAPT_COST` in the folder. **Dense products (C70, 2026-11-04; not yet run on a GPU): k-column kernels `mmvq-pascal-kcol*.cu`, fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-kcol` `e1fa4c573` (on the ladder branch), for Q8_0, Q2_0, Q3_K, Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K and IQ4_XS `MUL_MAT` of 1–8 tokens on compute 6.1, and the BF16 kernel (Q546) to 8 tokens.** Each weight is read and decoded once into int8 words and multiplied with dp4a by every column; the activations are quantized from FP32 (as `quantize_q8_1`) into records per lane unit and column (int8 values in each type's decode order, FP32 scales, the K-quants' block sums, Q3_K's/Q6_K's half-block offsets as the dp4a chains' initial values, bank-conflict padding) that each block stages in shared memory once: per 32 weights, row and column 8 dp4a and 3–5 FP32 operations. Weights staged per lane group (16-byte segments) or per warp from each row's 16-byte boundary (2-byte-aligned blocks, short rows). A column's sum depends on the row width only, the same for every column count. BF16: lanes write several outputs, so 4 rows per lane group serve 3–4 tokens (2 before) and 8 rows serve 5–8. Default on for 2–8 columns, plus one column for Q3_K (Q224) and Q8_0's short rows (Q604); `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_PASCAL_KCOL=0|1|2` and launch-shape overrides. 56–244 registers, no spills; each type's decoding matches ggml's reference dequantization on a CPU (`bench/mmvq_pascal/verify/kcol_unit_check`, 7/7 types, 240 cases each; a mutation fails exactly the types it touches). Est. 5-column cost against one column at memory speed: Q8_0, Q4_K–Q6_K ~1.0×, IQ4_XS ~1.1×, Q3_K ~1.4× (decode-bound past ~3 columns), BF16 ~1.0–1.2×. `bench/mmvq_pascal` `build/mmvq_kcol` (Flash-Next's dense shapes, per-pass totals), test-backend-ops cases at 1–8 tokens; the P106 run next (R16). New Q629–Q632. [data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_kcol_dense/README.md) **Routed experts (R7, 2026-11-04; not yet run on a GPU): `mul_mat_vec_q2_0_pascal_mtok`, fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-mtok` `c70687382` (on the ladder branch), takes every Q2_0 `MUL_MAT_ID` of 2–8 tokens on compute 6.1.** The entries that picked one expert form a group (found per warp with 8 ballots over the ids); its block reads the expert's rows once, looks each code word up once and multiplies it by every member, with the members' activations staged in shared memory once per block and 4 row blocks per block; lane groups, staging and sums are the one-token kernel's, so the results are bit-identical to k one-token passes. Fused gate/up + GLU, biases and the routed + shared launch as before; `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_PASCAL_MTOK=E,CAP,RB,G` turns it off or selects the group size, row blocks and gate loads. 76–168 registers, no spills. With 512 fine-grained experts the tokens of a pass share few experts (uniform routing: 47.7 distinct at k = 5; Flint-like locality est. ~41), so est. 70.7 → ~26–29 ms for the routed experts of a 5-token pass (6.1 ms at one token), still ~4–5× a token's. `bench/mmvq_pascal --q2_0-mtok` (k = 1–8, route profiles), test-backend-ops cases at 5–8 tokens and with overlapping routing; the P106 run and the in-model passes next (R7). The template renders the replies' text exactly (159,363 tokens against 159,598 generated; tool calls byte-exact). Data run 1's exact ids: 76 of 1,247 turns (6.1 %; 45 % of those over 4k tokens) change ids in a text round trip (`file` + `type` where the tokenizer writes `filetype`), 65 of them ≥ 256 tokens before the end, beyond the live ring at `-ub 128`. **Fix:** `LLAMA_SERVER_TEXT_REUSE=1` keeps the cached ids past the first difference for as long as both spell the same bytes (cut where a token ends in both), so the text is unchanged and the model reads its own ids. CPU check (tiny Flint, `-fa off`): 10 of 10 trials reuse prompt + reply (without it: the 18-token checkpoint and 744–1,177 tokens processed), answers equal to a cold server's on the same ids ([data](../docs/data/2026-10-28_reply_rereads/README.md)) | for the v3 release line, with Q263 (`pascal-p106-v3-promptpath` `8130a649d`); Q364 offers it upstream |

Depths 1–3 load with Q215's line; at depth 3 the pipe slots' compute buffers take 329.8 MiB on every card on top of the state groups, 3,869 MiB in all, and the fullest card holds 5,795 of 6,144 MiB (349 free; 116 MiB more per depth step). **The agent check at depth 3 (`cont`) against the same build without it:** 8 / 8 against 7 / 8 passed, 0.96× per task (41.2 against 42.3 minutes), but generation 4.9 against 7.9 tok/s; right guesses 29.7 % of the generated tokens and wrong 39.9 % (the model: 39.2 and 40.3 %), and a fit over the 40 requests gives +372 ms per wrong guess (~2.9 plain tokens), −145 ms per right one. The tool calls (23 % of the tokens) run unpipelined while the grammar is active (Q594). Host: MemAvailable lowest 138 against 227 MB, swap 1.48 against 0.85 GB; the first depth-3 arm was stopped by the harness's 150 MiB watchdog.
